```ts
import { TestBed } from '@angular/core/testing';

import { WwellThemeService } from './wwell-theme.service';

const SHELL_STORAGE_KEY = 'wwell-shell';
const SHELL_ATTRIBUTE = 'data-shell';

describe('WwellThemeService shell layout', () => {
  const root = document.documentElement;

  beforeEach(() => {
    localStorage.clear();
    root.removeAttribute(SHELL_ATTRIBUTE);
    TestBed.resetTestingModule();
  });

  it('defaults to the mono shell and stamps it on <html>', () => {
    const service = TestBed.inject(WwellThemeService);

    expect(service.shell()).toBe('mono');
    expect(service.isLegacyShell()).toBe(false);
    expect(root.getAttribute(SHELL_ATTRIBUTE)).toBe('mono');
  });

  it('switches to the legacy shell, updates <html> and remembers the choice', () => {
    const service = TestBed.inject(WwellThemeService);

    service.setLegacyShell(true);
    TestBed.tick();

    expect(service.shell()).toBe('legacy');
    expect(service.isLegacyShell()).toBe(true);
    expect(root.getAttribute(SHELL_ATTRIBUTE)).toBe('legacy');
    expect(localStorage.getItem(SHELL_STORAGE_KEY)).toBe('legacy');
  });

  it('restores a remembered legacy choice on the next visit', () => {
    localStorage.setItem(SHELL_STORAGE_KEY, 'legacy');

    const service = TestBed.inject(WwellThemeService);

    expect(service.isLegacyShell()).toBe(true);
    expect(root.getAttribute(SHELL_ATTRIBUTE)).toBe('legacy');
  });

  it('forgets the stored choice once the user returns to mono', () => {
    localStorage.setItem(SHELL_STORAGE_KEY, 'legacy');
    const service = TestBed.inject(WwellThemeService);

    service.setLegacyShell(false);
    TestBed.tick();

    expect(service.shell()).toBe('mono');
    expect(root.getAttribute(SHELL_ATTRIBUTE)).toBe('mono');
    expect(localStorage.getItem(SHELL_STORAGE_KEY)).toBeNull();
  });

  it('falls back to mono when the stored value is not recognised', () => {
    localStorage.setItem(SHELL_STORAGE_KEY, 'sepia');

    const service = TestBed.inject(WwellThemeService);

    expect(service.shell()).toBe('mono');
  });

  it('leaves the light/dark theme alone when the shell changes', () => {
    const service = TestBed.inject(WwellThemeService);
    const before = service.theme();

    service.setLegacyShell(true);
    TestBed.tick();

    expect(service.theme()).toBe(before);
  });
});

/* ── Switching light/dark ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 * The visible half is the browser's own default view-transition cross-fade — there are no keyframes of ours
 * to test. What the service owns is which path a switch takes, and the ordering the stylesheet depends on.
 */
describe('WwellThemeService theme switch', () => {
  const root = document.documentElement;
  const THEME_ATTRIBUTE = 'data-wp-theme';
  const CROSSFADE_CLASS = 'wp-theme-crossfade';
  const SWITCHING_CLASS = 'wp-theme-switching';

  /** Captures the colour-scheme listener so a test can drive an OS appearance change. */
  let fireSystemChange: ((dark: boolean) => void) | null = null;
  let startViewTransition: jest.Mock;

  const stubMedia = ({ dark = false, reducedMotion = false } = {}): void => {
    fireSystemChange = null;
    Object.defineProperty(window, 'matchMedia', {
      configurable: true,
      writable: true,
      value: (query: string) => {
        const isMotion = query.includes('prefers-reduced-motion');
        const listeners: ((e: { matches: boolean }) => void)[] = [];
        if (!isMotion) {
          fireSystemChange = (next: boolean) => listeners.forEach(fn => fn({ matches: next }));
        }
        return {
          matches: isMotion ? reducedMotion : dark,
          media: query,
          onchange: null,
          addListener: jest.fn(),
          removeListener: jest.fn(),
          addEventListener: jest.fn((_: string, fn: (e: { matches: boolean }) => void) => listeners.push(fn)),
          removeEventListener: jest.fn(),
          dispatchEvent: jest.fn(),
        } as unknown as MediaQueryList;
      },
    });
  };

  /** jsdom has no view transitions, so the primary path has to be stubbed in. */
  const installViewTransitions = (): void => {
    startViewTransition = jest.fn((update: () => void) => {
      update();
      return { finished: Promise.resolve(), ready: Promise.resolve(), updateCallbackDone: Promise.resolve() };
    });
    (document as unknown as Record<string, unknown>)['startViewTransition'] = startViewTransition;
  };

  beforeEach(() => {
    localStorage.clear();
    root.className = '';
    root.removeAttribute(THEME_ATTRIBUTE);
    root.style.removeProperty('--wp-theme-ms');
    stubMedia();
    installViewTransitions();
    TestBed.resetTestingModule();
  });

  afterEach(() => {
    delete (document as unknown as Record<string, unknown>)['startViewTransition'];
    delete (window as unknown as Record<string, unknown>)['matchMedia'];
    jest.restoreAllMocks();
  });

  it('cross-fades through a view transition when the theme changes', () => {
    const service = TestBed.inject(WwellThemeService);

    service.toggle();

    expect(startViewTransition).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
    expect(root.classList.contains(CROSSFADE_CLASS)).toBe(true);
    // The stylesheet's own transition must NOT also run, or the second snapshot would be taken mid-fade.
    expect(root.classList.contains(SWITCHING_CLASS)).toBe(false);
    expect(service.isDark()).toBe(true);
  });

  it('marks the root before starting the transition, not after', () => {
    // Load-bearing ordering. ArcGIS declares `:root { view-transition-name: none }`, and _wwell-theme.scss
    // only restores it under this class — so if the class landed after `startViewTransition()`, the root
    // would be captured nameless, there would be no pseudo-elements to fade, and the theme would just snap.
    let classAtStart: boolean | undefined;
    startViewTransition.mockImplementation((update: () => void) => {
      classAtStart = root.classList.contains(CROSSFADE_CLASS);
      update();
      return { finished: Promise.resolve(), ready: Promise.resolve(), updateCallbackDone: Promise.resolve() };
    });
    const service = TestBed.inject(WwellThemeService);

    service.toggle();

    expect(classAtStart).toBe(true);
  });

  it('flips the attribute inside the callback, so the second snapshot has the new theme', () => {
    const service = TestBed.inject(WwellThemeService);

    service.toggle();

    // Written synchronously rather than left to the effect, which would land after the snapshot is taken
    // and pop in once the fade was over.
    expect(root.getAttribute(THEME_ATTRIBUTE)).toBe('dark');
  });

  it('drops the class once the transition settles', async () => {
    const service = TestBed.inject(WwellThemeService);

    service.toggle();
    await Promise.resolve();
    await Promise.resolve();

    expect(root.classList.contains(CROSSFADE_CLASS)).toBe(false);
  });

  it('falls back to the stylesheet transition with no view-transition support', () => {
    delete (document as unknown as Record<string, unknown>)['startViewTransition'];
    const service = TestBed.inject(WwellThemeService);

    service.toggle();

    expect(root.classList.contains(SWITCHING_CLASS)).toBe(true);
    expect(root.classList.contains(CROSSFADE_CLASS)).toBe(false);
    expect(root.style.getPropertyValue('--wp-theme-ms')).toBe('250ms');
    expect(service.isDark()).toBe(true);
  });

  it('drops the fallback class only after its transition has had time to finish', () => {
    jest.useFakeTimers();
    delete (document as unknown as Record<string, unknown>)['startViewTransition'];
    const service = TestBed.inject(WwellThemeService);

    service.toggle();
    jest.advanceTimersByTime(250);
    expect(root.classList.contains(SWITCHING_CLASS)).toBe(true);
    jest.advanceTimersByTime(60);
    expect(root.classList.contains(SWITCHING_CLASS)).toBe(false);

    jest.useRealTimers();
  });

  it('does neither under reduced motion, but still changes the theme', () => {
    stubMedia({ reducedMotion: true });
    const service = TestBed.inject(WwellThemeService);

    service.toggle();

    expect(startViewTransition).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
    expect(root.classList.contains(SWITCHING_CLASS)).toBe(false);
    expect(service.isDark()).toBe(true);
  });

  it('does nothing visible when the pick lands on the theme already showing', () => {
    // Following the OS, which reports light — choosing Light explicitly changes the stored mode but repaints
    // nothing, and fading onto an identical picture reads as a glitch.
    const service = TestBed.inject(WwellThemeService);
    expect(service.theme()).toBe('light');

    service.setMode('light');

    expect(service.mode()).toBe('light');
    expect(startViewTransition).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
  });

  it('cross-fades when the OS appearance changes and we are following it', () => {
    // The default mode is "system", so this is the common case, not an edge one — and it used to set the
    // signal directly and skip the animation entirely.
    const service = TestBed.inject(WwellThemeService);
    expect(service.mode()).toBe('system');

    fireSystemChange?.(true);

    expect(service.theme()).toBe('dark');
    expect(startViewTransition).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
  });

  it('does nothing when the OS moves but an explicit theme is pinned', () => {
    const service = TestBed.inject(WwellThemeService);
    service.setMode('light');
    startViewTransition.mockClear();

    fireSystemChange?.(true);

    expect(service.theme()).toBe('light');
    expect(startViewTransition).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
  });
});
```

## [M] src/app/features/wwell-planning/shared/wwell-theme.service.ts

```ts
import { DOCUMENT } from '@angular/common';
import { ApplicationRef, Injectable, computed, effect, inject, signal } from '@angular/core';

export type WwellTheme = 'light' | 'dark';
/** What the user picked: a fixed theme, or `system` to follow the OS. */
export type WwellThemeMode = WwellTheme | 'system';
/** Which top bar + left nav is showing: the redesigned `mono` shell, or the navy `legacy` one it replaced. */
export type WwellShell = 'mono' | 'legacy';

const THEME_STORAGE_KEY = 'wwell-theme';
const SHELL_STORAGE_KEY = 'wwell-shell';
const SHELL_ATTRIBUTE = 'data-shell';
/** Dark keeps the key it shipped with, so a value saved before light gained a dial still applies. */
const CONTRAST_STORAGE_KEYS: Readonly<Record<WwellTheme, string>> = {
  dark: 'wwell-contrast',
  light: 'wwell-contrast-light',
};
const CONTRAST_PROPERTIES: Readonly<Record<WwellTheme, string>> = {
  dark: '--wp-contrast-dark',
  light: '--wp-contrast-light',
};
const THEMES: readonly WwellTheme[] = ['dark', 'light'];
const THEME_ATTRIBUTE = 'data-wp-theme';
/** Restores the root's view-transition-name for the length of the switch; see _wwell-theme.scss. */
const CROSSFADE_CLASS = 'wp-theme-crossfade';
/** Carries the stylesheet's own colour transition, for browsers with no view transitions. */
const SWITCHING_CLASS = 'wp-theme-switching';
const REDUCED_MOTION_QUERY = '(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)';
/**
 * The fallback cross-fade's duration, for browsers with no view transitions. Roughly what the browser's own
 * default transition runs to, so the swap feels the same either way. Short on purpose: the fallback transitions
 * colour on `*`, which repaints every element on screen for its whole length.
 */
const THEME_MS = 250;
const DARK_QUERY = '(prefers-color-scheme: dark)';
/** Slightly longer than the fade, so it finishes before the class — and with it the declaration — is dropped. */
const SWITCHING_MS = THEME_MS + 60;

export const MIN_CONTRAST = 0;
export const MAX_CONTRAST = 100;
/** Where each theme rests until the user moves its dial. Keep in step with the `var(--wp-contrast-*, N%)`
 *  fallbacks in _wwell-theme.scss. Light's standard look is its High end; dark opens softer than its own. */
export const DEFAULT_CONTRASTS: Readonly<Record<WwellTheme, number>> = { dark: 50, light: 100 };

/**
 * Light/dark theme for the wwell-planning module.
 *
 * Writes `data-wp-theme` and both contrast variables on <html> (not on a module element) because
 * dialogs, select panels and tooltips render on <body>, outside the module's DOM — they only pick up
 * the `--wp-*` tokens from _wwell-theme.scss by inheriting from the root. The tokens are `--wp-`
 * prefixed, so no other feature reacts to any of them.
 *
 * Follows the OS `prefers-color-scheme` until the user picks a theme; from then on the explicit
 * choice wins and is remembered in localStorage. Each theme has its own contrast dial (0 soft – 100
 * high, see `dark-tone` / `light-tone` in _wwell-theme.scss); both are always written, so switching
 * theme never waits on a second update to repaint at the right contrast.
 *
 * The same attribute mechanism carries the shell layout: `data-shell="mono" | "legacy"` on <html>
 * (see layout/_shell-theme.scss). Legacy is opt-in and remembered; mono is what everyone gets by default.
 *
 * Switching light/dark cross-fades: `commit()` runs the change inside a view transition and the browser fades
 * between its own before/after snapshots. Browsers without view transitions get the stylesheet's colour
 * transition instead.
 * See the switching section of _wwell-theme.scss.
 */
@Injectable({ providedIn: 'root' })
export class WwellThemeService {
  private readonly document = inject(DOCUMENT);
  private readonly appRef = inject(ApplicationRef);
  private readonly systemQuery = this.document.defaultView?.matchMedia?.(DARK_QUERY) ?? null;
  private readonly motionQuery = this.document.defaultView?.matchMedia?.(REDUCED_MOTION_QUERY) ?? null;

  /** The user's explicit pick; null means "follow the OS". */
  private readonly chosen = signal<WwellTheme | null>(this.readStoredTheme());
  private readonly system = signal<WwellTheme>(this.systemQuery?.matches ? 'dark' : 'light');
  private readonly contrasts = signal<Readonly<Record<WwellTheme, number>>>({
    dark: this.readStoredContrast('dark'),
    light: this.readStoredContrast('light'),
  });
  private readonly legacyChosen = signal<boolean>(this.readStored(SHELL_STORAGE_KEY) === 'legacy');
  private switchingTimer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | undefined;

  readonly shell = computed<WwellShell>(() => (this.legacyChosen() ? 'legacy' : 'mono'));
  readonly isLegacyShell = computed(() => this.legacyChosen());
  readonly theme = computed<WwellTheme>(() => this.chosen() ?? this.system());
  readonly isDark = computed(() => this.theme() === 'dark');
  readonly mode = computed<WwellThemeMode>(() => this.chosen() ?? 'system');
  /** Contrast of the theme that is showing, 0 (soft) – 100 (high). */
  readonly contrast = computed(() => this.contrasts()[this.theme()]);
  readonly isContrastDefault = computed(() => this.contrast() === DEFAULT_CONTRASTS[this.theme()]);

  constructor() {
    this.systemQuery?.addEventListener('change', (event) => {
      const next: WwellTheme = event.matches ? 'dark' : 'light';
      // Through `commit` like every other route in. Setting the signal directly meant that following the
      // OS — which is the DEFAULT mode, so the common case — repainted the whole app with no transition at
      // all: flip the appearance in system settings and the page simply snapped.
      //
      // It only repaints while we are actually following the OS. With an explicit theme picked, the OS
      // moving underneath changes `system()` but not `theme()`, and there is nothing to show.
      this.commit(() => this.system.set(next), this.chosen() === null && next !== this.theme());
    });

    // Applied synchronously so the first paint of a wwell-planning page is already in the right
    // theme; the effects keep the root in sync with later changes (toggle, OS switch, dial).
    this.apply(this.theme());
    this.applyContrasts(this.contrasts());
    this.applyShell(this.shell());
    effect(() => {
      this.apply(this.theme());
    });
    effect(() => {
      this.applyContrasts(this.contrasts());
    });
    effect(() => {
      this.applyShell(this.shell());
    });
  }

  toggle(): void {
    this.set(this.isDark() ? 'light' : 'dark');
  }

  set(theme: WwellTheme): void {
    this.setMode(theme);
  }

  setMode(mode: WwellThemeMode): void {
    if (mode === this.mode()) return;
    const chosen = mode === 'system' ? null : mode;
    // Picking "System" while it already resolves to the theme on screen changes nothing visible, so it gets
    // the plain commit — turning a page onto an identical one would just read as a glitch.
    const repaints = (chosen ?? this.system()) !== this.theme();
    this.commit(() => {
      this.chosen.set(chosen);
      this.writeStored(THEME_STORAGE_KEY, chosen);
    }, repaints);
  }

  /** Sets the contrast of the theme that is showing. */
  setContrast(value: number): void {
    const theme = this.theme();
    const next = clampContrast(value);
    if (next === this.contrasts()[theme]) return;
    this.contrasts.update((all) => ({ ...all, [theme]: next }));
    this.writeStored(CONTRAST_STORAGE_KEYS[theme], next === DEFAULT_CONTRASTS[theme] ? null : String(next));
  }

  resetContrast(): void {
    this.setContrast(DEFAULT_CONTRASTS[this.theme()]);
  }

  /** `true` shows the legacy navy shell; `false` is the default mono one. Independent of light/dark. */
  setLegacyShell(legacy: boolean): void {
    if (legacy === this.legacyChosen()) return;
    this.flagSwitching();
    this.legacyChosen.set(legacy);
    this.writeStored(SHELL_STORAGE_KEY, legacy ? 'legacy' : null);
  }

  private apply(theme: WwellTheme): void {
    this.document.documentElement.setAttribute(THEME_ATTRIBUTE, theme);
  }

  private applyShell(shell: WwellShell): void {
    this.document.documentElement.setAttribute(SHELL_ATTRIBUTE, shell);
  }

  private applyContrasts(all: Readonly<Record<WwellTheme, number>>): void {
    const style = this.document.documentElement.style;
    for (const theme of THEMES) {
      style.setProperty(CONTRAST_PROPERTIES[theme], `${all[theme]}%`);
    }
  }

  /**
   * Applies a theme change and shows it.
   *
   * `repaints` is false when the pick changes the stored mode but not what is on screen — choosing "System"
   * while it already resolves to the current theme. Transitioning onto an identical picture just reads as a
   * glitch, so that case commits silently.
   */
  private commit(change: () => void, repaints: boolean): void {
    if (!repaints || this.prefersReducedMotion()) {
      change();
      return;
    }

    if (typeof this.document.startViewTransition === 'function') {
      this.crossfade(change);
      return;
    }

    // No view transitions: the stylesheet's own colour transition instead.
    this.flagSwitching();
    change();
  }

  /**
   * Cross-fades to the new theme.
   *
   * The browser snapshots the page on either side of `change()` and fades between the two itself — no keyframes
   * of our own, which is the point: this is the default transition, the same one most products use for a theme
   * swap. See the switching section of _wwell-theme.scss for what the class is for.
   */
  private crossfade(change: () => void): void {
    const root = this.document.documentElement;
    // Before `startViewTransition`, not after: this class restores `view-transition-name: root` (ArcGIS
    // removes it), and it has to be there when the first snapshot is taken.
    root.classList.add(CROSSFADE_CLASS);

    const transition = this.document.startViewTransition(() => {
      change();
      // The attribute is written by an effect, and the second snapshot is taken as soon as this callback
      // settles — so everything signal-driven is flushed here, or it lands after that snapshot and pops in
      // once the fade is over.
      this.apply(this.theme());
      try {
        this.appRef.tick();
      } catch {
        // Already inside change detection. `apply()` above has put the theme on <html>, which is the part the
        // snapshot must have.
      }
    });

    const done = () => root.classList.remove(CROSSFADE_CLASS);
    transition.finished.then(done, done);
  }

  private prefersReducedMotion(): boolean {
    return this.motionQuery?.matches ?? false;
  }

  /** Adds the fallback transition's class for the length of the switch only, so it never runs on load. */
  private flagSwitching(): void {
    const root = this.document.documentElement;
    root.style.setProperty('--wp-theme-ms', `${THEME_MS}ms`);
    root.classList.add(SWITCHING_CLASS);
    clearTimeout(this.switchingTimer);
    this.switchingTimer = setTimeout(() => {
      root.classList.remove(SWITCHING_CLASS);
    }, SWITCHING_MS);
  }

  private readStoredTheme(): WwellTheme | null {
    const value = this.readStored(THEME_STORAGE_KEY);
    return value === 'light' || value === 'dark' ? value : null;
  }

  private readStoredContrast(theme: WwellTheme): number {
    const value = this.readStored(CONTRAST_STORAGE_KEYS[theme]);
    if (value === null) return DEFAULT_CONTRASTS[theme];
    const parsed = Number(value);
    return Number.isFinite(parsed) ? clampContrast(parsed) : DEFAULT_CONTRASTS[theme];
  }

  // Storage can throw (private mode, blocked site data). The appearance still works for the
  // session; it just isn't remembered — nothing the user needs to be told about.
  private readStored(key: string): string | null {
    try {
      return this.document.defaultView?.localStorage.getItem(key) ?? null;
    } catch {
      return null;
    }
  }

  /** `null` clears the key, so a choice equal to the default doesn't linger in storage. */
  private writeStored(key: string, value: string | null): void {
    try {
      const storage = this.document.defaultView?.localStorage;
      if (value === null) storage?.removeItem(key);
      else storage?.setItem(key, value);
    } catch {
      // See readStored: an unwritable store only costs persistence.
    }
  }
}

function clampContrast(value: number): number {
  return Math.min(MAX_CONTRAST, Math.max(MIN_CONTRAST, Math.round(value)));
}

```

## [M] src/app/features/wwell-planning/wwell-ir/wwell-ir-editor/wwell-ir-detail-rows.util.ts

```ts
import { IntegratedRequirement } from '@models/wwell-planning/integrated-requirement.model';

/** Flags a value that's actually meaningful, not just informational — e.g. a nonzero H2S
 *  reading should read as amber/danger regardless of which section it sits in. Omitted (or
 *  `'default'`) renders as a plain field. */
export type RowTone = 'default' | 'warning' | 'danger' | 'success';

export interface KvRow {
  readonly label: string;
  readonly value: string;
  readonly tone?: RowTone;
  /** Marks a value picked from a fixed set of options (a completion type, a test type, ...).
   *  A multi-select's picks arrive newline-separated in `value` and are shown one per line. */
  readonly options?: boolean;
  /** An identifier or a measurement (an IR number, a depth, a reading): set in the mono face, like the figures elsewhere. */
  readonly mono?: boolean;
}

export const EMPTY_VALUE = '—';

export function fmtNum(value: number | null | undefined, unit?: string): string {
  if (value === null || value === undefined) return EMPTY_VALUE;
  return unit ? `${value} ${unit}` : `${value}`;
}

export function fmtStr(value: string | null | undefined): string {
  return value ? value : EMPTY_VALUE;
}

/** `linkedRequestId` is the raw prewap_seq (e.g. "5008") — resolve it to the well name. Prefers
 *  `r.wellName` (populated from exad_rcd_prewap on a saved IR, or by the picker on a fresh one)
 *  over the "eligible for a new IR" wellOptions lookup: get_ir_wells_prc.sql's eligibility filter
 *  excludes any well that already has an IR header, so the instant this IR is saved its own
 *  linked well permanently drops out of that list and the lookup can never succeed again — the
 *  wellOptions param only still helps a brand-new, not-yet-saved draft. Falls back to the raw id
 *  if neither source has a name yet, rather than showing nothing. */
function resolveLinkedRequestLabel(
  linkedRequestId: string | null,
  wellName: string,
  wellOptions: readonly { readonly value: string; readonly label: string }[],
): string {
  if (!linkedRequestId) return EMPTY_VALUE;
  return wellName || wellOptions.find(option => option.value === linkedRequestId)?.label || linkedRequestId;
}

/** Mirrors the IR wizard's "Request Reference" step (wwell-ir-form.schema.ts) field-for-field,
 *  so the detail page's sections line up 1:1 with the stepper the data was captured through. */
export function buildRequestReferenceRows(
  ir: IntegratedRequirement,
  linkedRequestWellOptions: readonly { readonly value: string; readonly label: string }[] = [],
): readonly KvRow[] {
  const r = ir.requestReference;
  return [
    { label: 'Link to Request', value: resolveLinkedRequestLabel(r.linkedRequestId, r.wellName, linkedRequestWellOptions) },
    { label: 'Letter Number', value: fmtStr(r.letterNumber), mono: true },
    { label: 'BI Number', value: fmtStr(r.client) },
    { label: 'Supporting Business', value: fmtStr(r.supportingBusiness) },
    { label: 'Water Well Name', value: fmtStr(r.wellName) },
    { label: 'Target Aquifer', value: fmtStr(r.targetAquifer), options: true },
    // A brand-new draft is created through the required-number fallback in
    // mapIrFormValueToSubmission (targetDepthFt ?? 0) before the author has entered a real value —
    // treat a literal 0 as "not yet entered" rather than a misleading real reading (see the
    // matching fallback in mapIntegratedRequirementToFormPatch, wwell-ir-edit.mapper.ts).
    { label: 'Target Depth', value: r.targetDepthFt ? fmtNum(r.targetDepthFt, 'ft') : EMPTY_VALUE, mono: true },
    { label: 'Surface Elevation', value: `${fmtNum(r.surfaceElevationFtAsl)} ft ASL`, mono: true },
    { label: 'Coordinates', value: fmtStr(r.coordinates), mono: true },
  ];
}

/** Mirrors the schema's "Program Details" section (2.1) — its own rail leaf/edit unit, separate
 *  from Development & Testing (2.2) below. `openHoleSizeIn` only ever means something when the
 *  completion type is 'Open Hole' (see the field's `visibleWhen` gate in the schema), so it's
 *  only included then rather than showing a dangling "—" the rest of the time. */
export function buildProgramMainRows(ir: IntegratedRequirement): readonly KvRow[] {
  const p = ir.programDetails;
  const rows: KvRow[] = [{ label: 'Completion', value: fmtStr(p.completion), options: true }];

  if (p.completion === 'Open Hole') {
    rows.push({ label: 'Open Hole Size', value: fmtStr(p.openHoleSizeIn) });
  }

  rows.push(
    { label: 'Acidization', value: fmtStr(p.acidization), options: true },
    { label: 'Transmission', value: fmtStr(p.transmission), options: true },
  );
  return rows;
}

/** Mirrors the schema's "Development & Testing" section (2.2) — see buildProgramMainRows' doc
 *  comment for why this is a separate function rather than one combined list. */
export function buildProgramDevRows(ir: IntegratedRequirement): readonly KvRow[] {
  const d = ir.programDetails.developmentTesting;
  return [
    { label: 'Logging', value: fmtStr(d.logging), options: true },
    { label: 'Mud Logging', value: fmtStr(d.mudLogging), options: true },
    { label: 'Sampling', value: fmtStr(d.sampling), options: true },
    { label: 'Flow Test', value: fmtStr(d.flowTest), options: true },
    { label: 'Development/Testing', value: fmtStr(d.remarks) },
  ];
}

/** Mirrors the wizard's "Estimated Hydrology" step, including its test-type-conditional
 *  fields (SIWHP for a flow test; water level + pump depth for a pump test) — see the
 *  matching `visibleWhen` gates on `siwhpPsi`/`estimatedWaterLevelFt`/`estimatedPumpDepthFt`
 *  in wwell-ir-form.schema.ts. Any nonzero H2S gets `tone: 'warning'` — a toxic-gas reading is
 *  worth flagging on sight, not just listed at the same visual weight as TDS/rate. */
export function buildHydrogeologyRows(ir: IntegratedRequirement): readonly KvRow[] {
  const isFlowTest = ir.testProgram.testType === 'flow-test';
  const h2sPpm = ir.hydrogeology.h2sPpm;
  const rows: KvRow[] = [
    { label: 'Aquifer', value: fmtStr(ir.hydrogeology.aquifer), options: true },
    { label: 'Test Type', value: isFlowTest ? 'Flow Test' : 'Pump Test', options: true },
    { label: 'Estimated TDS', value: fmtNum(ir.hydrogeology.estimatedTdsPpm, 'ppm'), mono: true },
    { label: 'Estimated Rate', value: fmtNum(ir.testProgram.estimatedRateGpm, 'GPM'), mono: true },
    {
      label: 'H2S',
      value: fmtNum(h2sPpm, 'ppm'),
      tone: h2sPpm ? 'warning' : 'default',
      mono: true,
    },
  ];

  if (isFlowTest) {
    rows.push({ label: 'SIWHP', value: fmtNum(ir.testProgram.siwhpPsi, 'psi'), mono: true });
  } else {
    rows.push(
      { label: 'Estimated Water Level', value: fmtNum(ir.testProgram.estimatedWaterLevelFt, 'ft'), mono: true },
      { label: 'Estimated Pump Depth', value: fmtNum(ir.hydrogeology.estimatedPumpDepthFt, 'ft'), mono: true },
    );
  }
  return rows;
}

export function buildGeologistContactRows(ir: IntegratedRequirement): readonly KvRow[] {
  const c = ir.contacts;
  return [
    { label: 'Primary', value: fmtStr(c.geologistPrimary) },
    { label: 'Secondary', value: fmtStr(c.geologistSecondary) },
  ];
}
```

## [M] src/app/features/wwell-planning/wwell-ir/wwell-ir-editor/wwell-ir-editor.component.scss

```scss
@use '@angular/material' as mat;
@use '../../shared/wwell-select-panel-mixin' as wsel;
@use '../../shared/wwell-design-tokens' as tokens;
@use '../../shared/wwell-material' as wmat;

$navy: tokens.$navy-deep;
$muted: tokens.$muted;
$border: tokens.$border;
$accent: tokens.$accent;
$warning: tokens.$warning;

// `:host` is a no-op under ViewEncapsulation.None (see the component's own comment) — the tag
// selector is what actually reaches this element. Bounds the component's own height end to end
// so only `.ire__body` scrolls internally; header/rail-label/footer stay fixed chrome, same
// pattern as app-wwell-ir-detail (wwell-ir-detail.component.scss).
app-wwell-ir-editor {
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  height: 100%;
  min-height: 0;
  font-family: tokens.$font-family;
}

// Same rationale as wwell-ir-new-page/wwell-ir-detail's identical block — the app only calls
// mat.core() globally with no full M3 theme, so every Material control here (selects, inputs,
// toggle groups) needs its own scoped theme, including a select's portaled overlay panel (CDK
// renders it into <body>, hence the separate `.ire-select-panel` target).
.ire,
.ire-select-panel {
  color-scheme: var(--wp-scheme);

  @include mat.theme(
    (
      color: (
        theme-type: color-scheme,
        primary: mat.$blue-palette,
        tertiary: mat.$azure-palette,
      ),
      typography: (
        plain-family: tokens.$font-family,
        brand-family: tokens.$font-family,
      ),
      density: -4,
    )
  );
}

.ire {
  @include wmat.buttons;
  @include wmat.form-fields;
}

.ire-select-panel {
  border-radius: 10px !important;
  box-shadow:
    var(--wp-shadow-3) !important;
  @include wsel.panel($accent, $navy);
}

.ire,
.ire *,
.ire *::before,
.ire *::after {
  box-sizing: border-box;
}

.ire {
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  flex: 1;
  min-height: 0;
  gap: var(--wwell-gutter, 0.6rem);
  padding: 0;
  @include wsel.trigger($accent);
}

/* ── Header: fixed chrome, never scrolls ─────────────────────────────────── */
.ire__header {
  flex-shrink: 0;
  display: flex;
  align-items: center;
  justify-content: space-between;
  gap: 1rem;
  border: 1px solid transparent;
  border-radius: tokens.$radius-lg;
  padding: 0.7rem 1.1rem;
  @include tokens.glass-panel;
}

.ire__eyebrow {
  margin: 0 0 2px;
  font-size: tokens.$text-xs;
  font-weight: 600;
  letter-spacing: 0.02em;
  text-transform: uppercase;
  color: $accent;
  font-family: tokens.$font-mono;
  font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;
}

.ire__title {
  font-family: tokens.$font-display;
  margin: 0;
  font-size: tokens.$text-lg;
  font-weight: 650;
  line-height: 1.2;
  color: $navy;
  letter-spacing: -0.02em;
}

.ire__badge {
  display: inline-flex;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 6px;
  padding: 4px 12px;
  border-radius: 999px;
  font-size: tokens.$text-2xs;
  font-weight: 600;
  white-space: nowrap;
  flex-shrink: 0;
}

.ire__badge-dot {
  width: 6px;
  height: 6px;
  border-radius: 50%;
  flex-shrink: 0;
}

.ire__status-refresh-btn {
  flex-shrink: 0;
  width: 28px;
  height: 28px;
  padding: 0;
  line-height: 28px;

  ::ng-deep .mat-icon {
    font-size: 18px;
    width: 18px;
    height: 18px;
    line-height: 18px;
  }
}

.ire__header-actions {
  display: flex;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 0.6rem;
  flex-shrink: 0;
}

/* ── Sections/Timeline view toggle ────────────────────────────────
 * A plain segmented control — no @angular/material/button-toggle import needed for two states. */
.ire__view-toggle {
  display: inline-flex;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 2px;
  padding: 2px;
  background: tokens.$surface-alt;
  border: 1px solid $border;
  border-radius: 999px;
  flex-shrink: 0;
}

.ire__view-toggle-btn {
  display: inline-flex;
  align-items: center;
  justify-content: center;
  width: 28px;
  height: 28px;
  border: none;
  background: none;
  border-radius: 999px;
  padding: 0;
  color: $muted;
  cursor: pointer;
  transition:
    background-color 150ms ease,
    color 150ms ease;

  mat-icon {
    width: 16px;
    height: 16px;
    font-size: 16px;
  }

  &:hover {
    color: $navy;
  }

  &:focus-visible {
    outline: 2px solid $accent;
    outline-offset: 1px;
  }
}

.ire__view-toggle-btn--active {
  background: tokens.$surface;
  color: $accent;
  box-shadow: var(--wp-shadow-1);

  &:hover {
    color: $accent;
  }
}

.ire__tl-toggle-all-btn {
  width: 32px;
  height: 32px;
  padding: 0;
  flex-shrink: 0;
  color: $muted;

  &:hover {
    color: $accent;
  }

  mat-icon {
    width: 18px;
    height: 18px;
    font-size: 18px;
  }
}

.ire__tl-toggle-all-btn--active {
  color: $accent;
  background: tokens.$accent-soft;
  border-radius: 50%;
}

/* ── Body: IR SECTIONS rail + the always-rendered form ───────────────────── */
.ire__body {
  flex: 1;
  min-height: 0;
  display: flex;
  align-items: flex-start;
  gap: var(--wwell-gutter, 0.7rem);
  overflow-y: auto;
  overscroll-behavior-y: contain;
}

/* ── Rail ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 * A plain nested nav list, not the old wizard's absolutely-positioned chip timeline — nothing
 * here needs to track a panel's live Y-position (see SectionRailController's doc comment: every
 * section is always rendered, so there's no accordion resize to chase). `position: sticky`
 * against `.ire__body` (its scrolling ancestor) is enough to keep it in view.
 */
.ire__rail {
  // One vertical rhythm for the whole nav. Every row is the same height and every consecutive
  // pair is separated by the same gap, whether or not a group boundary falls between them —
  // previously the gap ran 0 / 0.8 / 2.4 / 5.6px depending on where you were in the tree, which
  // fused each group's children together and opened a void before the next group. Hierarchy is
  // carried by indent, the connector line and type weight instead.
  --rail-gap: 0.15rem;
  flex-shrink: 0;
  position: sticky;
  top: 0;
  align-self: flex-start;
  width: 196px;
  max-height: 100%;
  overflow-y: auto;
  padding: 0.65rem 0.5rem;
  background: tokens.$surface-alt;
  border: 1px solid $border;
  border-radius: 10px;
  box-shadow:
    var(--wp-shadow-1);
}

.ire__rail-title {
  margin: 0 0 0.4rem 0.4rem; // matches a row's 0.4rem content inset (3px border + padding)
  font-size: tokens.$text-2xs;
  font-weight: 650;
  letter-spacing: 0.08em;
  text-transform: uppercase;
  color: $muted;
}

.ire__rail-list,
.ire__rail-children {
  list-style: none;
  margin: 0;
  padding: 0;
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  gap: var(--rail-gap);
}

.ire__rail-group-btn,
.ire__rail-child-btn {
  display: flex;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 0.5rem;
  width: 100%;
  border: none;
  border-left: 3px solid transparent;
  background: none;
  border-radius: 7px;
  padding: 0.32rem 0.4rem 0.32rem calc(0.4rem - 3px);
  font: inherit;
  text-align: left;
  cursor: pointer;
  color: $navy;
  transition:
    background-color 150ms ease,
    border-color 150ms ease,
    color 150ms ease;

  &:hover {
    background: tokens.alpha($accent, 8%);
  }

  &:focus-visible {
    outline: 2px solid $accent;
    outline-offset: -2px;
  }
}

.ire__rail-group-dot {
  width: 6px;
  height: 6px;
  border-radius: 50%;
  background: $muted;
  flex-shrink: 0;
}

.ire__rail-number {
  flex-shrink: 0;
  font-weight: 650;
  font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;
  font-size: tokens.$text-2xs;
  color: $muted;
}

.ire__rail-label {
  font-size: 0.84rem;
  font-weight: 600;
  overflow: hidden;
  text-overflow: ellipsis;
  white-space: nowrap;
}

.ire__rail-group-btn--active,
.ire__rail-child-btn--active {
  background: tokens.alpha($accent, 12%);
  border-left-color: $accent;

  .ire__rail-number,
  .ire__rail-label {
    color: $accent;
  }

  .ire__rail-group-dot {
    background: $accent;
  }
}

.ire__rail-children {
  // Top margin matches the list gap so stepping from a group header into its first child costs
  // the same as any other step; no bottom margin, so the parent list's own gap alone separates
  // the last child from the next group.
  margin: var(--rail-gap) 0 0 1.3rem;
  padding-left: 0.4rem;
  border-left: 1px solid $border;
}

.ire__rail-child-btn {
  // No padding override: the shared rule above already carries the row height and the
  // `calc(0.4rem - 3px)` that compensates for the transparent 3px active-marker border. The
  // shorthand that used to sit here dropped that compensation, pushing every child's text 3px
  // off the indent grid, and made child rows 2.5px shorter than group rows.
  .ire__rail-label {
    font-weight: 500;
  }
}

/* ── Sections column ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */
.ire__sections {
  flex: 1;
  min-width: 0;
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  gap: 0.65rem;
  padding-bottom: 0.5rem;
}

.ire__section,
.ire__group {
  border: 1px solid transparent;
  border-radius: tokens.$radius-lg;
  padding: 0.7rem 0.9rem;
  @include tokens.glass-panel;
  // Keeps a scrolled-to section's heading clear of the sticky app chrome above `.ire__body`.
  scroll-margin-top: 0.5rem;
}

.ire__group {
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  gap: 0;
}

.ire__group .ire__section--sub {
  border: none;
  border-radius: 0;
  // Inherited from `.ire__section` above; a sub-section is only a divided region of its group,
  // so its own shadow drew a faint box edge around each one.
  box-shadow: none;
  background: transparent;
  border-top: 1px solid $border;
  margin: 0.65rem 0 0;
  padding: 0.65rem 0 0;

  &:first-of-type {
    margin-top: 0.5rem;
    border-top: none;
    padding-top: 0;
  }
}

.ire__section-title {
  font-family: tokens.$font-display;
  position: relative;
  margin: 0 0 0.5rem;
  padding-left: 0.65rem;
  font-size: 0.95rem;
  font-weight: 650;
  color: $navy;

  &::before {
    content: '';
    position: absolute;
    left: 0;
    top: 0.1em;
    bottom: 0.1em;
    width: 3px;
    border-radius: 999px;
    background: $accent;
  }
}

.ire__group > .ire__section-head {
  margin-bottom: 0;
}

/* ── Timeline view ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
 * `.ire__tl-row`/`.ire__tl-rail`/`.ire__tl-content` wrap every top-level section unconditionally
 * in the template (see wwell-ir-editor.component.html) so switching view modes never re-creates
 * a section's SectionEditState or its scroll-spy anchor. In "sections" mode (the default) they
 * collapse to `display: contents` — invisible to layout — so today's plain `.ire__sections` flex
 * list renders exactly as before. Only `.ire__sections--timeline` turns them into an actual
 * dot + connecting-line stepper, using the same light-surface tokens as the rest of the page. */
.ire__tl-row,
.ire__tl-content {
  display: contents;
}

.ire__tl-rail {
  display: none;
}

.ire__body--timeline {
  align-items: stretch;
}

.ire__sections--timeline {
  padding-left: 0.1rem;

  .ire__tl-row {
    display: flex;
    align-items: stretch;
    gap: 0.65rem;
  }

  .ire__tl-rail {
    position: relative;
    display: flex;
    justify-content: center;
    width: 22px;
    flex-shrink: 0;

    &::after {
      content: '';
      position: absolute;
      top: 22px;
      bottom: -0.65rem;
      left: 50%;
      width: 2px;
      background: $border;
      transform: translateX(-50%);
    }
  }

  .ire__tl-row:last-child .ire__tl-rail::after {
    display: none;
  }

  .ire__tl-content {
    display: block;
    flex: 1;
    min-width: 0;
  }

  .ire__section-head,
  .ire__group > .ire__section-head {
    cursor: pointer;
    user-select: none;
    border-radius: 4px;

    &:focus-visible {
      outline: 2px solid $accent;
      outline-offset: 2px;
    }
  }
}

/* ── Timeline preview panel ────────────────────────────────────────
 * The Wellbore Preview isn't tied to any one lifecycle step, so Timeline view pins it here
 * instead of leaving it in the scrolling step list — `position: sticky` against `.ire__body`
 * (its scrolling ancestor), same pattern the Sections rail already uses, so it stays in view no
 * matter which step is open or how far the steps are scrolled. It's a normal flex sibling of the
 * steps column, never `position: fixed` — it must never sit as a floating overlay on top of the
 * steps content, only ever occupy its own reserved width alongside it.
 *
 * Hiding it animates that reserved `width` down to 0 (rather than removing it from the DOM, or
 * just fading it in place) specifically so the steps column visibly reclaims the freed space as
 * it collapses, instead of leaving a blank gap where the panel used to be. The inner
 * `.ire__tl-preview-anim` wrapper holds its own width fixed and fades/slides out on a faster,
 * independent transition, so the content is already gone by the time the outer collapse would
 * otherwise start clipping it mid-shape.
 */
.ire__tl-preview-sidebar {
  // Grows with the page (the diagram, its figures and labels scale with it), between the old fixed 320px and 27rem.
  --ire-preview-w: clamp(20rem, 27vw, 27rem);

  flex-shrink: 0;
  // A column, so the card inside can be told to fill exactly what the page leaves it (a percentage height would
  // have nothing definite to resolve against here, which is what let the card run past the fold and get clipped).
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  position: sticky;
  top: 0;
  align-self: flex-start;
  width: var(--ire-preview-w);
  // As tall as the page leaves it, whatever the card inside is: the card is told to fill this, so a height taken
  // from the card's own content would be circular (and used to let the card run past the fold and get clipped).
  height: 100%;
  overflow: hidden;
  transition: width tokens.$duration-base tokens.$ease-out;

  &.ire__tl-preview-sidebar--hidden {
    width: 0;
  }
}

.ire__tl-preview-anim {
  flex: 1;
  min-height: 0;
  width: var(--ire-preview-w, 20rem);
  overflow-y: auto;
  overflow-x: hidden;
  transition:
    opacity tokens.$duration-fast tokens.$ease-out,
    transform tokens.$duration-fast tokens.$ease-out;
}

.ire__tl-preview-sidebar--hidden .ire__tl-preview-anim {
  opacity: 0;
  transform: translateX(16px);
}

@media (max-width: 1100px) {
  // Only the resting (open) width shrinks here — the hidden-state override above always collapses
  // to 0 regardless of breakpoint, so it isn't repeated in this media query.
  .ire__tl-preview-sidebar {
    --ire-preview-w: 260px;
  }
}

.ire__tl-dot {
  position: relative;
  z-index: 1;
  display: flex;
  align-items: center;
  justify-content: center;
  width: 22px;
  height: 22px;
  margin-top: 0.1rem;
  border-radius: 50%;
  background: tokens.$surface;
  border: 2px solid $border;
  flex-shrink: 0;

  mat-icon {
    width: 13px;
    height: 13px;
    font-size: 13px;
    color: tokens.$surface;
  }
}

.ire__tl-dot--done {
  background: var(--wp-success-fill);
  border-color: var(--wp-success-fill);
}

.ire__tl-dot--active {
  border-color: $accent;
  box-shadow: 0 0 0 3px tokens.alpha($accent, 16%);
}

// The cobalt ring marks the step still to do. Timeline view opens every step, so a completed one is
// also "active" — without this it took the active ring and halo over its green fill and read as
// half-done. Done wins: green fill, green ring, green halo.
.ire__tl-dot--done.ire__tl-dot--active {
  border-color: var(--wp-success-fill);
  box-shadow: 0 0 0 3px tokens.alpha(var(--wp-success-fill), 22%);
}

.ire__tl-status {
  flex-shrink: 0;
  font-size: tokens.$text-2xs;
  font-weight: 600;
  color: $muted;
  padding: 0.1rem 0.6rem;
  border-radius: 999px;
  background: tokens.$surface-alt;
  border: 1px solid $border;
  white-space: nowrap;
}

.ire__tl-status--done {
  color: var(--wp-success);
  background: var(--wp-success-soft);
  border-color: var(--wp-success-line);
}

.ire__subsection-title {
  margin: 0 0 0.4rem;
  font-size: 0.86rem;
  font-weight: 600;
  color: $navy;
}

/* ── Per-section inline edit ──────────────────────────────────────
 * Each section flips independently into an editable state (its own pencil button, its own
 * Save/Cancel row) — any number of sections can be mid-edit at once, since each one owns its
 * own form/edit-state instance (see SectionEditState in wwell-ir-section-edit.util.ts) rather
 * than sharing one page-level "editing" flag. */
.ire__section-head {
  display: flex;
  align-items: center;
  justify-content: space-between;
  gap: 0.5rem;
  margin: 0 0 0.5rem;

  .ire__section-title,
  .ire__subsection-title {
    margin: 0;
  }
}

.ire__edit-btn {
  width: 28px;
  height: 28px;
  padding: 0;
  flex-shrink: 0;
  color: $muted;

  &:hover {
    color: $accent;
  }

  mat-icon {
    width: 16px;
    height: 16px;
    font-size: 16px;
  }
}

.ire__edit-btn--danger:hover {
  color: var(--wp-danger-ink);
}

/* Groups a section-head's edit + reset buttons so `.ire__section-head`'s `justify-content:
 * space-between` sees exactly two children (title, actions) instead of spreading the buttons
 * apart from each other across the row. */
.ire__section-actions {
  display: flex;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 0.1rem;
  flex-shrink: 0;
}

.ire__edit-actions {
  display: flex;
  justify-content: flex-end;
  gap: 0.5rem;
  margin-top: 0.65rem;
}

/* A "+" button's one-row add form: its own card above the table, matching the table wrapper's border and radius. */
.ire__add-block {
  margin-bottom: 0.65rem;
  padding: 0.85rem 0.9rem 0.7rem;
  border: 1px solid $border;
  border-radius: 8px;
}

/* ── View-mode read display ───────────────────────────────────────
 * `[data-tone]` (bound from KvRow.tone — e.g. a nonzero H2S reading) flags a field whose value
 * is actually a problem, not just data. Values are plain text — no pill or box around them; a
 * multi-select's picks are listed one per line by `app-wwell-ir-kv-value`. */
.ire__view-grid {
  margin: 0;
  display: grid;
  grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(160px, 1fr));
  gap: 0.65rem 1.25rem;
}

.ire__view-field {
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  gap: 0.15rem;
  min-width: 0;

  dt {
    font-size: tokens.$text-2xs;
    font-weight: 600;
    color: $muted;
  }

  dd {
    margin: 0;
    font-size: 0.86rem;
    font-weight: 600;
    color: $navy;
    // A remarks field's saved value can carry real line breaks (e.g. Development/Testing,
    // Sampling) — without this, `\n` renders as a plain space and a 3-line remark collapses
    // back into one run-on line.
    white-space: pre-line;
  }

  &[data-tone='warning'] dd {
    color: $warning;
  }

  &[data-tone='danger'] dd {
    color: var(--wp-danger-ink);
  }

  &[data-tone='success'] dd {
    color: var(--wp-success);
  }
}

.ire__view-table-wrap {
  overflow-x: auto;
  border: 1px solid $border;
  border-radius: 8px;
}

.ire__view-table {
  width: 100%;
  border-collapse: collapse;
  font-size: 0.82rem;
  white-space: nowrap;

  th {
    background: tokens.$surface-alt;
    color: $muted;
    font-weight: 600;
    font-size: tokens.$text-2xs;
    text-transform: uppercase;
    letter-spacing: 0.04em;
    text-align: left;
    padding: 0.4rem 0.6rem;
    border-bottom: 1px solid $border;
  }

  td {
    padding: 0.4rem 0.6rem;
    color: $navy;
    border-bottom: 1px solid var(--wp-line-soft);
  }

  tr:last-child td {
    border-bottom: none;
  }
}

.ire__view-table-empty {
  text-align: center;
  color: $muted;
  font-style: italic;
}

.ire__view-table-actions-col {
  width: 1%;
}

.ire__row-actions {
  display: flex;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 0.15rem;
  white-space: nowrap;
}

.ire__row-action-btn {
  width: 26px;
  height: 26px;
  padding: 0;
  flex-shrink: 0;
  color: $muted;

  &:hover {
    color: $accent;
  }

  mat-icon {
    width: 15px;
    height: 15px;
    font-size: 15px;
  }
}

.ire__row-action-btn--danger:hover {
  color: var(--wp-danger-ink);
}

.ire__remarks,
.ire__empty-text {
  margin: 0;
  font-size: 0.86rem;
  color: $muted;
  font-style: italic;
}

/* ── Attachments (7.1 Location Map / 7.2 Initiation Request) ─────────────────── */
/* The slot's own icon, inline in its subsection heading — this is the one visual cue that tells
 * the two slots apart at a glance, since their empty states are otherwise identical. */
.ire__attachment-slot-icon {
  width: 16px;
  height: 16px;
  font-size: 16px;
  margin-right: 0.35rem;
  vertical-align: -3px;
  color: $accent;
}

.ire__attachment-required {
  margin-left: 0.2rem;
  color: var(--wp-danger-ink);
  font-weight: 600;
}

.ire__attachment-error {
  display: flex;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 0.4rem;
  margin: 0 0 0.75rem;
  padding: 0.5rem 0.7rem;
  border: 1px solid var(--wp-danger-line);
  border-radius: 8px;
  background: var(--wp-danger-soft);
  color: var(--wp-danger-ink);
  font-size: 0.82rem;
  font-weight: 600;

  mat-icon {
    width: 16px;
    height: 16px;
    font-size: 16px;
  }
}

/* Empty slot: hint copy plus one explicit Upload button. The card itself is deliberately NOT the
 * click target — with a real button present, a whole-card hit area just makes stray clicks open a
 * file dialog the user didn't ask for. */
.ire__attachment-drop {
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  align-items: center;
  justify-content: center;
  gap: 0.25rem;
  padding: 1.1rem 1rem;
  border: 1.5px dashed $border;
  border-radius: 10px;
  background: tokens.$surface-alt;
  text-align: center;
  transition:
    border-color 150ms ease,
    background 150ms ease;

  &:focus-within {
    border-color: $accent;
  }

  > mat-icon {
    width: 22px;
    height: 22px;
    font-size: 22px;
    color: $accent;
  }
}

/* Icon-only upload control — a round accent button, sized to match the row actions on an occupied
 * slot so the two states read as the same control set rather than two different designs. */
.ire__attachment-upload-icon {
  display: inline-flex;
  align-items: center;
  justify-content: center;
  width: 38px;
  height: 38px;
  margin-top: 0.6rem;
  border-radius: 50%;
  background: tokens.$cta-bg;
  color: tokens.$on-cta;
  cursor: pointer;
  transition:
    background 150ms ease,
    opacity 150ms ease;

  &:hover {
    background: tokens.$cta-bg-hover;
  }

  /* Keyboard users reach the label through its hidden input, so the ring has to be drawn here. */
  &:focus-within {
    outline: 2px solid $accent;
    outline-offset: 2px;
  }

  &--busy {
    cursor: progress;
    opacity: 0.65;
  }

  mat-icon {
    width: 20px;
    height: 20px;
    font-size: 20px;
  }
}

/* Skinny by design — this sits under a compact card, so a full-height Material bar would read as a
 * separate element rather than as feedback belonging to the slot above it. */
.ire__attachment-progress {
  margin-top: 0.5rem;
  height: 3px;
  border-radius: 999px;
  overflow: hidden;
}

.ire__attachment-drop-label {
  font-size: 0.82rem;
  font-weight: 600;
  color: $navy;
}

.ire__attachment-drop-hint {
  font-size: tokens.$text-2xs;
  color: $muted;
}

/* Occupied slot: file identity on the left, actions on the right. */
.ire__attachment-file {
  display: flex;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 0.55rem;
  padding: 0.55rem 0.65rem;
  border: 1px solid $border;
  border-radius: 10px;
  background: tokens.$surface-alt;

  /* Held for an unsaved New IR — dashed, matching the empty slot's border, so "not committed yet"
   * is visible at a glance rather than only in the caption text. */
  &--pending {
    border-style: dashed;
    border-color: $accent;
  }
}

.ire__attachment-file-icon {
  flex: none;
  width: 20px;
  height: 20px;
  font-size: 20px;
  color: $accent;
}

.ire__attachment-file-meta {
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  min-width: 0;
  /* Takes the slack so the action buttons stay pinned right regardless of name length. */
  flex: 1 1 auto;
}

.ire__attachment-file-name {
  font-size: 0.84rem;
  font-weight: 600;
  color: $navy;
  overflow: hidden;
  text-overflow: ellipsis;
  white-space: nowrap;
}

.ire__attachment-file-size {
  font-size: tokens.$text-2xs;
  color: $muted;
}

.ire__attachment-action {
  flex: none;
  display: inline-flex;
  align-items: center;
  justify-content: center;
  width: 30px;
  height: 30px;
  border-radius: 50%;
  color: $muted;
  cursor: pointer;

  &:hover {
    color: $accent;
    background: var(--wp-surface-hover);
  }

  &--danger:hover {
    color: var(--wp-danger-ink);
  }

  /* The one action that actually commits the file, so it carries accent weight from the start
   * rather than only on hover like the neutral view/replace icons beside it. */
  &--upload {
    color: $accent;

    &:hover:not(:disabled) {
      background: var(--wp-surface-hover);
    }
  }

  mat-icon {
    width: 17px;
    height: 17px;
    font-size: 17px;
  }
}


/* ── Casing size warnings — same treatment as the old wizard's ──────────────── */
.ire__casing-warnings {
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  gap: 0.5rem;
  margin: 0.75rem 0 0;
  padding: 0;
  list-style: none;
}

.ire__casing-warning {
  display: flex;
  align-items: flex-start;
  gap: 0.55rem;
  padding: 0.6rem 0.85rem;
  background: var(--wp-warning-soft);
  border: 1px solid tokens.alpha($warning, 25%);
  border-radius: 10px;
  font-size: 0.84rem;
  line-height: 1.4;
  color: $warning;

  mat-icon {
    flex-shrink: 0;
    width: 18px;
    height: 18px;
    font-size: 18px;
    margin-top: 0.1rem;
  }
}

/* ── Footer: fixed chrome, never scrolls ─────────────────────────────────── */
.ire__footer {
  flex-shrink: 0;
  display: flex;
  justify-content: flex-end;
  gap: 0.6rem;
  padding: 0.5rem 0.8rem;
  border: 1px solid transparent;
  border-radius: tokens.$radius-lg;
  @include tokens.glass-panel;
}

// Keeps the Save button's own width stable whether it's showing the save icon or the
// in-flight spinner, so the footer never reflows/jumps the instant a save starts.
.ire__save-btn .mdc-button__label {
  display: inline-flex;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 0.4rem;
}

.ire__save-spinner {
  display: inline-flex;
}

// Sits opposite Save/Submit for Review — `margin-right: auto` claims the footer's leading edge
// against its `justify-content: flex-end`, so a destructive action is never adjacent to Save and
// can't be misclicked in its place.
.ire__delete-btn {
  margin-right: auto;

  .mdc-button__label {
    display: inline-flex;
    align-items: center;
    gap: 0.4rem;
  }
}

.ire .mat-mdc-form-field-required-marker {
  color: var(--wp-danger);
}

// Material dims disabled fields to 38% alpha, which is near-invisible on the field's grey fill.
// Wellbore Details locks fields that carry the linked well's data (BI Number, Target Aquifer…)
// and users need to read them, so they get the view-mode look: navy value, muted label, semibold.
.ire__section--wellbore .mat-mdc-form-field {
  .mat-mdc-input-element,
  .mat-mdc-select-value {
    font-weight: 600;
  }

  &.mat-form-field-disabled {
    .mat-mdc-input-element,
    .mat-mdc-select-value,
    .mat-mdc-form-field-text-suffix {
      color: $navy;
      -webkit-text-fill-color: $navy;
    }

    .mat-mdc-floating-label {
      color: $muted;
    }
  }
}

/* ── Tighter field density, scoped to this component only ───────────────────
 * The shared dynamic-form building blocks (dyf__field, dys__grid, rpg__row) are reused by other
 * consumer forms (wwell-request-form, wwell-wlda-request-form) with their own spacing needs, so
 * their own stylesheets are left untouched — these ::ng-deep overrides only apply where `.ire`
 * wraps them, keeping the "skinny" treatment local to the IR editor. */
.ire ::ng-deep {
  .dys {
    margin-bottom: 0.65rem;
  }

  .dys__grid {
    gap: 0.5rem 0.85rem;
  }

  .rpg {
    gap: 0.45rem;
  }

  .rpg__row {
    padding: 0.5rem 0.65rem;
    border-radius: 9px;
  }

  .rpg__row-fields {
    gap: 0.4rem 0.7rem;
  }

  .rpg__row-index {
    width: 1.3rem;
    height: 1.3rem;
    margin-top: 0.2rem;
  }
}

@media (max-width: 900px) {
  .ire__body {
    flex-direction: column;
    overflow-y: visible;
  }

  .ire__rail {
    position: static;
    width: 100%;
    max-height: none;
  }

  .ire__rail-children {
    margin-left: 1.2rem;
  }

  // `.ire__body` just switched to a column flex — everything stacks full-width, so a width
  // collapse (the desktop hide/show mechanism above) can't reclaim any space here. `order: -1`
  // keeps it above the steps list, same placement it always had; the hidden state below collapses
  // `max-height` instead, since that's the axis that actually frees up room in a stacked layout.
  .ire__tl-preview-sidebar {
    position: static;
    width: 100%;
    height: auto;
    max-height: none;
    overflow: hidden;
    order: -1;
    transition: max-height tokens.$duration-base tokens.$ease-out;

    &.ire__tl-preview-sidebar--hidden {
      width: 100%;
      max-height: 0;
    }
  }

  .ire__tl-preview-anim {
    width: 100%;
    max-height: none;
  }
}

@media (max-width: 640px) {
  .ire {
    padding: 0.6rem;
  }

  .ire__footer {
    flex-wrap: wrap;

    button {
      flex: 1;
    }
  }
}
```

```ts
} from '../wwell-ir-attachments.config';
import { WwellIrService } from '../wwell-ir.service';
import { WWELL_IR_STATUS_META, WWELL_IR_SUBMITTABLE_STATUSES, hasKnownIrStatus } from '../wwell-ir.config';
import {

} from './wwell-ir-detail-rows.util';
import { WWELL_IR_PREVIEW_DIAGRAM_LAYOUT, WWELL_IR_SIDEBAR_DIAGRAM_LAYOUT } from './wwell-ir-preview-diagram-layout';
import {
```
